Tech > Home Entertainment > TV Accessories
The fourth-gen Apple TV 4K is now three times the price of Roku's top streamer.
Editor
Editor
October 13, 2026, 9:48 am ET
Apple has unveiled its latest streaming box, the Apple TV 4K (fourth generation), which has a faster processor and onboard microphones for better integration with Siri AI. It’s coming in November for $299.
The new Apple TV has an updated A19 chip, which Apple says is 50% faster than the existing A15 processor and has twice the graphics performance. The TV 4K boasts 128GB of storage space which is the same as the previous flagship.  
The new set-top box has three onboard far-field microphones for conversing with Siri, as well as a familiar-looking remote with a traditional microphone button. The remote also includes Find My with Precision Finding if it gets lost behind the cushions.
More from CNET
Connectivity includes:
All of the relevant formats are included, such as Dolby Atmos, Dolby Vision and HDR10 Plus. You can now also create a four-speaker surround system by pairing the Apple TV 4K (fourth generation) and the new HomePod Mini 2. 
At $299, though, the Apple TV is more expensive than every other premium streaming box on the market. For instance, it’s $200 more than the Roku Ultra and the (seemingly discontinued) Google TV. The outgoing Apple TV 4K (2022) recently had a price hike of its own, with the entry-level model leaping from $129 to $199, and the company blamed the increase on an electronics shortage also known as RAMageddon.
While it’s the most expensive streamer, the fourth-gen Apple TV 4K is also likely to be capable, especially if you’re a gamer. The outgoing TV 4K was great for streaming games via Steam Link or Xbox Remote Play, and the new box should be even better. For everyone else, who simply want to watch TV, a $50 streamer from Amazon or Roku should do just fine.
The new Apple TV 4K is available now for preorder at Apple’s website. It’s due in stores on Nov. 13.

Home > Smart Home > Smart Speakers
Apple's new smart display and speaker marks the company's Siri AI-ification of your home. Preorders are open now.
Principal Writer
Principal Writer
October 13, 2026, 9:13 am ET
Apple has welcomed an entirely new product type to its ever-growing family of devices. The company unveiled the HomeView smart speaker at its Welcome Home event in New York on Tuesday — a long-rumored addition that looks like a hybrid between a HomePod and a giant Apple Watch.
The HomeView’s square screen is a 7-inch Liquid Retina display, making it quite unobstrusive. In spite of its diminutive size, this bold design choice will set Apple’s smart speaker apart from most rivals on the market, such as the Amazon Echo Show, which tend to have rectangular landscape screens.
On the front of the HomeView, a 12-megapixel camera uses Apple’s Center Stage technology — a zoom feature designed for video chatting, which you might be familiar with from its iPad lineup. Inside the display, two full-range drivers will help deliver room-filling Spatial Audio. The device is powered by Apple’s homegrown A18 chip — the same silicon inside the iPhone 16 Series and the MacBook Neo.
More from CNET
Apple’s home hub functions as a central portal for entertainment, connection and controlling your smart home devices, but also for interacting with Apple Intelligence and Siri AI. This builds on the Siri AI revamp that Apple announced back at WWDC in June, which saw the company introduce a more capable, conversational voice assistant that matches up-to-date AI smarts with the company’s privacy and security standards.
“It is little surprise that the HomeView coincides with Apple’s renewed push on Siri AI and Apple Intelligence,” said Ben Wood, chief analyst at FDM CCS, speaking from the company’s New York event. “The company has undoubtedly been waiting for the upgraded Siri experience, given how important the voice interactions are with this product.”
Unlike most Apple devices, which are either designed completely for personal use (the iPhone or the Apple Watch) or designed to be shared by everyone in the home (the Apple TV or HomePods), the HomeView straddles both worlds. “Apple HomeView is designed to be shared by everyone in the home yet feel completely personal to each user,” said Greg Joswiak, Apple’s senior vice president of Worldwide Marketing in a press release. If you don’t already use them, it sounds like it might be time to tap into shared photo albums and shared Apple Music playlists in order to make the most of the HomeView.
Apple’s HomeView will recognize your family members using built-in facial recognition, and will allow you all to check your personal content in Messages, FaceTime, Calendar, Reminders and Find My. Furthermore, when paired with an Apple Intelligence-enabled device, HomeView will draw on your personal context to provide personalized answers to any questions you might ask.
Your HomeView device can either sit on your tabletop or be mounted to a wall, depending on your preference. One thing to note is that you have to decide this when you buy — either you pick the wall-mounted option or one with a domed speaker base attached. If you opt for the wall-mounted version, it can be attached using three purpose-built mounts from Schneider Home, with the option to install it yourself or have a professional do it for you.
A collection of playful and informative faces is available — some with your personal photos, others with an animated Snoopy. These faces respond dynamically to your physical and presence and movement, which sounds like a fun feature for a kitchen dance party. The idea is true personalization — whether you want interactive art, a functional interface or something in between.
Customers in the US, Australia, Canada, India, New Zealand and the UK can preorder the HomeView right now for $299, with devices available from Nov. 13. It comes in silver, starlight, rose pink and space gray, so there’s bound to be at least one option that will fit your home decor scheme.

Tech > Mobile > Phones > Foldable Phones
Save your preferred phone color, storage, mobile carrier and payment method in advance so you can quickly complete your purchase on Friday.
Senior Writer, Electrified Cars
Senior Writer, Electrified Cars

				Edited by Gael Cooper 
October 12, 2026, 8:27 pm ET
If you’re eager to get your hands on the highly anticipated iPhone Duo, the first foldable iPhone, here’s how to get a head start on ordering. Apple has opened a staging page for preorders on apple.com, where you can get ready to place a preorder for the viral phone.
Head over to Apple’s online storefront, and you’ll find that a new iPhone Duo configurator is now live. On that page, save your preferred phone color, storage, mobile carrier and payment method. Saving these details in advance ensures you can complete your purchase immediately when preorders open at 5 a.m. PT on Friday.
Apple unveiled the iPhone Duo, one of its first new products under the helm of new CEO John Ternus, during its Surprise and Shine event in Cupertino, California, on Friday. It’s the first foldable from Apple, although merely the latest in a wave of new bendy phones to hit the market.
The iPhone Duo opens to reveal a 7.6-inch display and folds down to a pocketable passport size. The shifting shape supports split-screen multitasking and Apple Pencil, and brings a host of new features and animations to Apple iOS 27.
Pricing starts at $1,999 for the 512GB model and tops out at $3,199 for the 2TB version — before carrier subsidies — making the iPhone Duo the most expensive iPhone ever released. Price aside, observers expect demand among early adopters to be high, so prepping your order now is a smart idea.
Not quite sure that the new iPhone Duo is the right phone for you? I’m also on the fence. Check out CNET’s coverage leading up to the launch of the new foldable and iOS 27, as well as the more conventionally shaped iPhone 18 Pro, so you can make an informed decision.

24,2026-10-13,"It stopped building the ID.4 in April and halted ID. Buzz imports, while European EV share hit 29%
The Volkswagen assembly plant in Chattanooga, Tennessee, photographed in September 2025.
Volkswagen sold 1,421 electric cars in the United States in the third quarter, a 90% fall, after ending ID.4 production and halting imports of its electric minivan. In Europe battery electric cars took 29% of new registrations in August.
Volkswagen sold 1,421 electric cars in the United States between July and September. That is 90% fewer than a year earlier.
The ID.4 accounted for 989 of them, down 92.1% from 12,470, and the ID. Buzz for 432, down 82.5%, InsideEVs reported. Total US sales fell 10.9% to 78,179.
This is a supply number, not a demand one. For most of this year Volkswagen has been selling whatever was left on dealer lots, and InsideEVs calls it among the biggest drops of any electric carmaker in the market.
The ID.4 left production in Chattanooga in April, so the plant could build the petrol Atlas instead, and ID. Buzz imports stopped, leaving only 2025 cars.
Europe went the other way. Battery electric cars took 29% of new registrations across the continent in August, and Volkswagen is now taking more orders for electric cars than petrol and diesel in Germany.
Four budget models built in Spain have drawn about 100,000 orders between them. Volkswagen cancelled extra shifts at Wolfsburg as combustion demand fell, and added them at Emden and Zwickau.
Its European problem is a different one. Chinese brands took a record 10.9% share of the European market in June, roughly double their share a year before.
That is costing Volkswagen its best sellers. The Tiguan plug-in hybrid went from Europe’s top-selling plug-in to fourth in a year, behind the BYD Seal U.
Chief executive Oliver Blume wants Brussels to act, saying there is no time to lose. Existing EU tariffs of up to 35% cover battery electric cars from China but not plug-in hybrids.
The American replacement borrows the name. The ID. Tiguan arrives next year with a front boot and more physical controls, and the minivan returns as a 2028 model with a native NACS port.
Until then the lots are the lineup. The ID.4 has been discounted by up to $12,500, while the ID. Buzz still starts at $61,545 for a range topping out at 234 miles.
Washington removed the help. The ID.4 sold 248 cars in the final quarter of last year, once the federal tax credit expired, and tariffs contributed to a 53% drop in Volkswagen’s profit.
The contrast is sharper in China. Volkswagen’s joint venture there sells a plug-in hybrid saloon from about $17,175, under a third of what the minivan costs in America, with no plan to bring it west.

25,2026-10-13,"OrbitronAI’s co-founders, with CEO Saulius Adomaitis seated in front
Aramco Digital, the tech arm of Saudi oil giant Aramco, has teamed up with start-up OrbitronAI to build and sell AI tools for heavy industry, the two firms said.
The tools are aimed at oil and gas, power, mining, transport, and factory firms. They are meant to help them find money lost to slow decisions, scattered data, and heavy paperwork in big projects, supplier deals, and daily work.
The deal was signed in September at the LEAP 2026 tech show in Riyadh, Middle East AI News reported. Neither firm gave financial terms.
OrbitronAI makes software that uses AI agents to read company files, connect to business systems, and perform tasks. Key sums and sign-offs stay with people, the firms said.
The two will first build tools with customers around real cases. They will then test them and turn the ones that work into products they can sell to the wider industry.
“Through our partnership with OrbitronAI, we intend to convert that industrial advantage into scalable AI products for Aramco, our affiliates and the wider industrial market,” said Ashraf Tahini, chief executive of Aramco Digital.
The work will start in Saudi Arabia, with plans to sell the products in other countries later.
“Together with Aramco Digital, we are building a repeatable model for Industrial Value Recovery,” said Saulius Adomaitis, OrbitronAI’s co-founder and chief executive.
Adomaitis used to lead EY’s global oil and gas business, the firm said. His co-founders are Luvy Singh, who ran supply chains at Shell and other big firms; Ashu Gupta, the chief AI officer; and Poonam Gupta, who runs engineering.
Aramco has been spending heavily on AI. Its venture arm backed Together AI’s $800 million funding round in July. Other start-ups are chasing the same market: Freehand raised $75 million in July for supply-chain AI agents that negotiate with suppliers.

27,2026-10-13,"South Korea's stock market plunged on Tuesday as investors found a new reason to worry about the AI trade.
The benchmark Kospi index fell more than 7% intraday to its lowest level since July 31, extending a dramatic reversal for what was once the world's hottest AI-fueled stock market.
AI chip heavyweights Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix led the losses, falling 9% and 10%, respectively, by midday. 
The weakness spread across Asia, with Japan's Nikkei and Taiwan's chip-heavy Taiex both falling around 3%. The declines followed another weak session for US semiconductor stocks overnight, including Micron.
Investors were weighing a growing list of concerns about the AI trade, from rising Chinese competition to questions over how the industry's massive infrastructure buildout will be financed.
""The latest catalyst has been further evidence of China's growing ambitions across the AI ecosystem,"" Chris Weston, the head of research at Pepperstone, wrote in a note Tuesday.
Weston pointed to Chinese memory-chip maker CXMT, which said it had made progress in developing technology that could pack more data onto memory chips and that it plans to introduce a new server-memory product by year-end.
CXMT has yet to demonstrate it can manufacture the technology at scale, and the announcement does not immediately challenge South Korea's lead in high-bandwidth memory chips used in AI accelerators. 
But it has added to investor concerns over how long memory makers can maintain their pricing power.

Tech > Services & Software > AI
Gemini 4 Argon is now available for paying subscribers and businesses.
Reporter 2
Reporter 2
October 13, 2026, 12:01 pm ET
When Google DeepMind announced Gemini 4 Argon a few weeks ago, the lab said it would be limiting initial access to the new AI model to cybersecurity defenders. Now it’s loosening the reins and letting paying subscribers get their hands on the new Gemini.
Gemini 4 Argon is available globally for enterprises and for Google AI Ultra and Pro subscribers in the Gemini app. It’s also available in Android Studio, Google AI Studio and the agentic developer platform Google Antigravity. Take note of the introductory pricing. Access begins at $2 per million input tokens and $10 per million output tokens, but that rises to $4 per million input tokens and $20 per million output tokens after Nov. 30.
Google says that it has used Gemini 4 internally to help with software engineering, cybersecurity and research. In a blog post, the company says Gemini 4 has been used to optimize Python codebases, identify and patch cybersecurity holes and make its open Gemma AI models three times quicker at tasks. The AI also played a role in developing physical AI, like Google’s robotics research, helping ground a humanoid device in the real world’s physics and understand its surroundings.
More from CNET
It seems like Google is trying to market Gemini 4 as a hard-core, serious companion for STEM workers. We’re far away from the days of Nano Banana — Google’s AI image generator that blew its competition out of the water and enraged creators. Now Google is focusing on both practical and abstract applications of AI for math and science, like robotics and nuclear fusion research. 
It’s part of a bigger trend we’ve seen across the AI industry. As criticism of AI and the companies building it mounts — from the anger over data center construction to the questions raised by AI agents making unwanted incursions — Big Tech leaders have pivoted to speak about AI’s ability to cure cancer, for example.
Gemini 4 Argon competes with frontier models like OpenAI’s GPT-6 Astra and Meta’s new Muse Wonder. Google had the edge among AI companies for most of 2025, but advancements from other labs left it mostly behind for this year. Also throughout the past year, concerns about AI safety have risen significantly, particularly around “AI doomsday” scenarios. 
Google says that it engaged with the White House’s voluntary review process — which the public has no insight into —for new models and that Gemini 4 passed inspection on its safety framework. Only time will tell if Google’s precautions are enough to prevent myriad AI-powered harms.

30,2026-10-13,"According to a new report from IDC, Apple’s share of smartphone shipments in China grew during Q3 2026, despite a 4.3% year-over-year decline in iPhone shipments. Here are the details.
In a new report released today, IDC says that rising component costs and retail prices continue to weigh on the broader Chinese smartphone market.
According to IDC, Apple accounted for 17.8% of smartphone shipments in China during Q3 2026, up from 15.8% in the year-ago period. The gain came despite a 15.4% year-over-year decline in overall smartphone shipments in China, with Apple itself seeing a 4.3% year-over-year decline and a 15.1% drop from the previous quarter.
This means that while Apple lost ground, it didn’t lose as much as most of its competitors, thus increasing its overall shipment share.
And speaking of Apple’s competitors, Huawei was the uncontested winner for the quarter, with a massive 17.3% year-over-year increase in shipments, bringing its market share to 21.1%, up from 15.2% in Q3 2025. However, its shipments still declined 17.6% from the previous quarter.
Other than Huawei, Lenovo was the only other brand in China’s top 10 to record year-over-year shipment growth, at 1.1%. On a quarter-over-quarter basis, however, Lenovo, Honor, and Samsung all recorded increases, with Samsung posting a whopping 106.5% jump.
On the other end of the spectrum, Wiko saw the biggest year-over-year decline among the top 10, with shipments plunging 74% in Q3 2026, bringing its shipment share down to 0.8%, from 2.6% in the year-ago period.
Here’s IDC on these numbers, which it notes are preliminary (translation ours):
Despite the overall market downturn, Huawei maintained its strong performance throughout the year, supported by its diverse product lineup and relatively restrained price increases. Its shipments recorded double-digit year-over-year growth.
Apple’s newly released iPhones received a better-than-expected market reception. However, shipments declined slightly year-over-year due to fewer models available this year.
Other major Chinese smartphone brands faced significant challenges but continued to demonstrate varying degrees of resilience under pressure.
Among them, Honor was the only major manufacturer to record quarter-over-quarter shipment growth, suggesting a potential rebound.
IDC says it expects component costs “ to remain elevated over the next several quarters,” which will in turn keep increasing device prices and stretch replacement cycles further as consumers “accept and adapt to higher prices.”
The company doesn’t expect cost pressures to subside “through much of 2027, with the market likely to continue experiencing significant declines.”

31,2026-10-13,"The Brexit debate is back.
For years, it felt like the only thing Westminster talked about.
I have had a ringside seat at it all, from the Conservative election victory in 2015, with the party's promise in its manifesto that year to offer ""a straight in-out referendum on our membership of the European Union by the end of 2017.""
Then there was the then Prime Minister David Cameron's negotiation with the European Union to try to secure what he saw as a better deal and then the referendum itself on Thursday, 23 June 2016.
And that, you will remember, was merely the start, as years of wrangling began. Two general elections came and went in 2017 and 2019 and it took the two prime ministers after David Cameron, first Theresa May and then Boris Johnson, before the UK left the European Union at 23:00 on Friday, 31 January 2020.
Little wonder perhaps that by that point, almost irrespective of someone's views on the topic, there was an appetite to talk about something else. Anything else. Political parties picked up that vibe and while their long standing instincts on the EU were there to see if you scratched the surface, there was minimal desire to extemporise about them any time soon.
This video can not be played
Burnham: Post-Brexit status quo with Europe 'not good enough for Britain'
With the arrival of Andy Burnham as prime minister, that has changed, despite what he said just a few months before he arrived in Downing Street. 
He has been happy to repeatedly lean into an argument claiming that it is time to ""face up to the reality that Brexit has done more harm than good"" and ""the status quo is not good enough for Britain."" First he made this case at the Labour Party Conference last month and now he has made it again in the House of Commons.
Burnham is attempting to make a two-pronged case. 
First, in the short term, he wants to ""deepen"" the UK's relationship with the EU at a summit in Brussels at the end of next month. He insists such a deepening would be within the promise made by Labour at the last general election that the UK would say no to the customs union, the single market and freedom of movement. 
Which brings us onto the second element of his case, in the medium term. What might Labour promise in its next general election manifesto? The prime minister told me a fortnight ago the manifesto could promise an in-out referendum.
In the Commons, he sought to reassure you, whatever your view on Brexit. 
""This country in the next decade needs to know where it's going. There are no assumptions here, and as I said, the British public will be the boss of whatever is decided,"" he said.
Hang on, say critics, it was decided by voters a decade ago. 
""They weighed the arguments and made a decision. The least they are owed is politicians willing to defend it,"" argues Lord Frost, external, a former Brexit minister and chief Brexit negotiator in the Telegraph.
The focus of the next five weeks, ahead of a UK-EU Summit in Brussels on Friday 20 November, is what Downing Street sees as short term wins on youth mobility, food and drink, and energy.
Andy Burnham hopes to make an argument that what he will portray as a sensible, pragmatic deepening of the relationship with Brussels can help cut illegal immigration and boost economic growth.
He sought to goad the Conservative leader Kemi Badenoch for seeking to oppose a plan he thinks can contribute to growth. She argued it risked regurgitating yesterday's arguments with little prospect of success.
And, strikingly, his attempt at goading his opponents went further still. He told The Rest Is Politics podcast that upon arriving in Downing Street, he had asked if there was Russian interference in the Brexit referendum. 
""I've had a clear answer that, yes, there was,"" he claimed, without saying who provided that clear answer or what the evidence for that suggested clarity was.
In the Commons, his language appeared a little softer. He told MPs it was ""inconceivable"" to think that Russia did not try to interfere.  A double negative deployed this time, about alleged attempts at interference.
His political opponents were already pouncing. The Reform UK leader Nigel Farage was blunt â calling the prime minister ""a liar.""
The former Prime Minister Boris Johnson added that he had asked the same question as Andy Burnham did when he became Foreign Secretary, external ""and they replied that there was no evidence of any successful interference in the 2016 referendum, by Russia or any other power"".
This video can not be played
Watch: PM says there is 'clear' evidence of Russian interference in 2016 Brexit campaign
And so a debate resumes about the difference between attempted interference, interference and successful interference.
But the key point is this: for the first time since the UK left the European Union, we now have a prime minister who is willing to unflinchingly debate Brexit: whether it is working, how it is working, how to improve it, and if it's proved to be a failure.
In so doing he hopes he can tap into public sentiment. Opinion polls suggest a perception among a majority that leaving the European Union has not turned out how many might have hoped.
On top of this, he hopes to lure pro Europeans broadly on the Left who may recently have supported the Liberal Democrats, the Green parties of both England and Wales and Scotland, the Scottish National Party and Plaid Cymru towards Labour. That is the political logic from the government's point of view, for now at least.
And as for where all of this leads â well, good question. In the coming weeks, months and perhaps years, there may be a Back to the Future vibe to our political debate. A splash of familiarity, as a conversation about the relationship we have with our nearest neighbours resumes.

33,2026-10-13,"
This as-told-to essay is based on a conversation with Joanna Strober, the 58-year-old cofounder and CEO of Midi Health, a virtual care platform dedicated to modernizing women's healthcare. She is based in Los Altos Hills, California. This story has been edited for length and clarity.
I'm the founder and CEO of Midi Health. We started the company to bridge the gap in healthcare by providing specialized, insurance-covered care for women in their 30s, 40s, 50s, and 60s.
For many years, I didn't have time to take care of myself. Taking care of three kids and working was not easy. It was all-encompassing.
I had a big wake-up call when I was 44 years old and had a small fall and fractured my shoulder. I discovered that I had osteopenia, or low bone density, at a very young age. That was a good indicator that I needed to focus more on prevention and taking care of myself. Now I'm making up for the lost time.
I wake up and have two Nespresso pods and two pieces of Levain bread from a bakery called Manresa Bread. It's a very dense multigrain loaf that fills you up. For the past 10 years, I've had this bread every single morning for breakfast, or as soon as the bakery opens.
Then I walk for about an hour. That is the most important part of my routine. I listen to podcasts about politics or AI. I like ""Hard Fork,"" ""The Daily,"" ""The Ezra Klein Show,"" and ""Call Me Back.""
The goal is to lift weights after my walk, but it doesn't always happen. I go to the office after, and then I have lots of Zoom calls and product meetings.
I try to schedule another 45-minute walk around lunchtime. I take a lot of phone calls and have been told I talk loudly when I walk. It's actually a joke in my neighborhood.
I mostly drink chamomile tea in the afternoon, but I also let myself have one Diet Coke per day.
I take Midi's Omega-3+, which also has vitamin D and K in it, and that's for my bones because I have osteopenia. I also take creatine and fiber. I either take the Midi fiber gummies or I just make sure I get enough fiber.
I also use hormone replacement therapy to help prevent bone deterioration.
I have a salad or a sandwich for lunch, or sometimes I walk somewhere to get lunch.
For many years, my life was structured around getting my kids breakfast or dinner. Now they're no longer home, and it's actually very liberating. It means I can work much later and have fewer guardrails.
I usually get dinner between 6:30 and 7 p.m. Sometimes I'll pick up a random dinner on the way home or make pasta. Or, I'll eat ice cream for dinner.
I don't drink or do drugs. I love British detective TV shows. I find it very relaxing to watch TV and eat ice cream. I buy low-sugar blackberry jam from the farmers market and eat it with chocolate ice cream every night.
Create a BI Job Scout and get opportunities matched to your goals.
Take a smarter break in your day - and see how far you get.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
I'm asleep by 9:30 p.m.
In general, I've been trying different meditation apps. I can't say it's working, but I am trying.
Walking is my best route to sanity. I walk over 10,000 steps every day, but it's less about step count and more about making sure my body moves for multiple hours. My morning walk is in the hills, but when I walk flat, I try to wear a weighted vest.
Every weekend, I walk The Dish at least twice, and sometimes three or four times. It's our local hike in Palo Alto. It's about three-and-a-half miles, and it's uphill. I also schedule at least one or two walks a day with different friends for social activity.
On weekends, I also go to the farmers market and cook.
When I was younger and had children, my mom and I made a deal that she would cook dinner for my family every Friday night. Now the kids are gone, and she still cooks dinner for my husband and me every Friday.
It's the one night a week I know I'm going to get good food, and someone is going to take care of me. I feel really lucky.
I don't want to be crippled as an older lady. I've also watched what it's like to have someone with Alzheimer's in my family, and I'm scared of that. I think a lot about what I can do to stay healthy and prevent heart disease, Alzheimer's, and bone disease.
I don't track macros or anything like that, but I am very neurotic about my cancer screenings and the behaviors that are easy to do to make sure that I stay healthy as long as I can — like sleeping enough, eating the right foods, and getting the right exercise.
I think about what I can do to make sure I can eventually run after my grandkids.

34,2026-10-13,"Delta Air Lines is delaying the launch of its new route to Saudi Arabia's capital, Riyadh, following recent Houthi missile attacks on the city's airport.
The route, which would have started in Atlanta, was scheduled to begin on October 23 with a week of daily service before moving to three times a week. It was set to be the first non-stop flight between the US and Saudi Arabia.
""Saudi Arabia remains an important market for Delta, and we are committed to serving the Kingdom for the long-term,"" the airline said. 
Delta said the flights will now start in March 2027, though it did not specify an exact start date.
""The safety and security of our customers and employees always come first and continue to guide our operational decisions,"" Delta said in its announcement. ""We remain in close contact with relevant government and aviation partners, including Saudia and Riyadh Air, as we monitor the situation.""
Riyadh's King Khalid International Airport has suffered a number of attacks in recent weeks.
On Saturday, hundreds were wounded, and 13 people were killed by a missile strike. The following day, another strike killed one person and injured about a dozen.
The Houthis, who are backed by Iran, have been attacking Saudi Arabia after gaining control of a key strait in the Red Sea.
Delta's decision came after the Federal Aviation Administration issued a warning for US airlines on Sunday.

The device consists of a 7-inch Liquid Retina display that sits atop a domed speaker base and works with Siri AI. 36,2026-10-13,"A Pro model adds eye tracking, iris payments, and health tracking, and Alibaba also launched earbuds made with Bose.
Alibaba’s Qwen Glasses N1 series and Qwen Clip earbuds
Alibaba has started selling its new Qwen Glasses N1 smart glasses in China, at prices from 2,999 yuan ($450) before discounts, Bloomberg reported from a launch event in Hangzhou on Tuesday.
Several Chinese outlets put the starting price slightly lower, at 2,899 yuan. With state subsidies and launch offers, buyers can pay as little as 2,218 yuan, according to Yahoo Finance Hong Kong. The glasses are on sale on Taobao, Tmall, JD.com, and Douyin.
The N1 is no cheaper than Meta’s Ray-Ban glasses, which lead the market, Bloomberg noted. Like Meta, Alibaba wants glasses to become a way into its AI tools. Meta added a camera-free model to its range last month.
The N1 has a 50-megapixel camera and runs Alibaba’s Qwen AI assistant, which users talk to through bone-conduction microphones.
It can translate, take meeting notes, help with shopping and payments, and carry out tasks by voice.
A single charge gives 25 hours on standby or nine hours of music, according to Bloomberg, and Chinese reports said the battery can be swapped.
The N1 Pro, priced at 3,499 yuan, adds eye tracking. Wearers can look at an object to ask about it, and confirm payments with an iris scan.
The Pro also tracks heart rate, blood oxygen, and skin temperature, Chinese tech site PConline reported.
Alongside the glasses, Alibaba began selling Qwen Clip, its first AI earbuds. Made with Bose, they clip onto the ear and cost 1,299 yuan, according to Bloomberg. They also translate and take notes.
Alibaba first showed the N1 series and the earbuds at its Apsara conference in September, and opened pre-orders on September 22.
At the launch, Song Gang, a vice president in Alibaba’s Qwen business, described the glasses as a second pair of eyes and ears for users, Chinese site ZOL reported.
Glasses makers in China also have new guidance to follow. In September, China issued a voluntary code asking AI glasses to show a light when they record.

37,2026-10-13,"The Osaka facility could cost up to ¥1tn ($6.3bn) to build and draw up to 500 megawatts, Nikkei Asia reports.
NTT Data Group is close to agreeing to a deal with Anthropic for a new data centre in Osaka, Nikkei Asia reported on Tuesday, citing multiple sources.
It would be the first site in Japan built just for Anthropic, which makes the Claude AI models. TNW has not independently confirmed the report, and both NTT Data and Anthropic declined to comment to Nikkei.
Construction alone could cost up to ¥1 trillion ($6.3 billion), according to Nikkei. Adding a large number of AI servers with graphics processors would bring the total bill to several trillion yen.
The site would draw up to 500 megawatts of power, making it one of the largest data centres in Japan. The two companies aim to sign a deal by the end of March 2027, the close of Japan’s fiscal year, with part of the site opening as early as 2028.
Power is the main hurdle. Drawing 500 megawatts from the grid would be slow, so the project will first look at other options, such as generating electricity on site and using fuel cells, Nikkei reported.
NTT Data, part of the telecoms group NTT, ranks third among the world’s data centre operators by market share. It runs more than 1,600 megawatts of capacity in several countries.
Anthropic already has a foothold in Japan. In May, Hitachi agreed to roll out Claude across its workforce of about 290,000 people.
The company is also looking at new data centres in other Asian countries, where governments are competing to host them, according to Nikkei. In Australia, the Australian Financial Review has reported that it is in talks with local firms.
Japan is courting such projects as part of a wider data centre push. The government wants ¥32.7 trillion of public and private money in data centres, cloud computing, and battery storage by 2035. Other large sites are planned in Akita and Kagoshima, Nikkei reported.

38,2026-10-13,"Ithaca Holdings is in advanced talks to buy the dating app, whose shares jumped about 50% on Monday.
Bumble founder Whitney Wolfe Herd at TechCrunch Disrupt in San Francisco, September 2018.
Ithaca Holdings is in advanced talks to buy Bumble and take the dating app private, in a deal that would value it at about $1 billion. Reuters reported the talks, citing one source familiar with the matter.
We have not independently confirmed the report, and neither company replied when Reuters asked for comment.
Investors moved fast, and on Monday, Bumble’s shares jumped about 50% to close at $3.80, marking their biggest single-day gain since the company went public. Before this, Bumble’s market value was about $389 million as of Friday’s close.
Ithaca, an investor in tech companies, was started by Silicon Valley executives Ravi Gupta and John Hegeman.
Earlier this year, Reuters said Bumble was considering a sale because growth in online dating had slowed. Both Bumble and Match Group, which owns Tinder and Hinge, have faced challenges as younger users lose interest in swiping.
Bumble’s data highlights the pressure. In 2025, paying users fell by over 11% to about 3.7 million, and annual sales dropped nearly 10% to around $966 million. By the second quarter of 2026, paying users had fallen 16.4% to 3.2 million.
Whitney Wolfe Herd started Bumble in 2014 with the idea that only women could send the first message. When Bumble went public on the Nasdaq in 2021, it was valued at $14 billion.
After Wolfe Herd came back as CEO in March 2025, Bumble worked to bring users back. That month, it launched an AI dating assistant called Bee, which chooses matches for users instead of having them swipe.
In August, Bumble changed its main rule, allowing men to send the first message. The company also cut 30% of its staff in mid-2025.
Neither company has shared when a deal might be signed or what it would look like. If it happens, Bumble’s five years as a public company would come to an end.

39,2026-10-13,"Commodities are having a moment, and headlines will tell you why: the impact of the wars in Iran and Ukraine-Russia on energy markets, the AI boom's reliance on a wide range of rare earth minerals, and long-term inflation and sovereign debt concerns bolstering precious metals.
The Bloomberg Commodity Index has returned nearly 32% year to date, and some investors are taking notice.
""Hot money attracts assets,"" said Mark Hackett, chief market strategist of Nationwide Investment Management, noting that commodities' recent strength may be coming at the expense of the bond market.
""I personally think a lot of this sell-off that we've seen in bonds in the last couple of weeks is people throwing in the towel on bonds,"" Hackett said. He expects to hear this exact logic parroted on TikTok and X as a reason to buy commodities, though he cautioned it's not ""wise.""
That's not to say he isn't bullish on commodities for the longer term. He wrote an article in September making the case for commodity exposure in portfolios, citing their relative lack of correlation with equity markets. They can be a way to play long-term trends while protecting against market downturns in a way that bonds don't seem to do anymore, he said.
While Hackett sees value in commodities investments, he cautioned against making short-term trades. As any commodities trader will tell you, history is littered with firms that fell apart from a failed commodity bet.
""There's a large graveyard of people who thought they were smart enough to bet on specific moves in energy prices,"" Hackett said. Here's how he suggests investors approach the asset class.
Don't try to time the market, because ""commodities don't always act the way you think they should,"" Hackett said.
""If you try and pick the top and the bottom of commodities, you're asking for trouble,"" Hackett said, noting that the wide range of traders and investors betting on it means that you are ""running against a fickle crowd.""
Take a smarter break in your day - and see how far you get.

                                    Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                  
Look no further than the chaos in oil markets since the US began its war in Iran this February. Investors buying large amounts of oil in March, expecting long-term disruption to oil prices due to the blockade of the Strait, would have had quite a rough summer as prices fell back from their initial surge.
It may be a fool's errand to try to bet on what happens over the next three to six months in commodities, but there are clear long-term trends pushing prices up.
In developed countries, the AI boom is dramatically increasing the demand for power and minerals such as copper. In developing countries, rising living standards require more commodities. Increased meat consumption globally is a long-term driver of corn and wheat prices, which are used to feed livestock. Farmers need 10 calories of grain to produce a calorie of beef.
There's also a portfolio-construction reason: bonds have been moving in the same direction as equities since 2022, and investors are looking for alternatives to their equity holdings.
""This is the period where you would want bonds to act as a ballast,"" Hackett said. Instead, they're acting like equities with worse returns.
But that doesn't mean you should sell all of your bonds to load up on commodities right now, especially while bonds are cheap and commodities are expensive, Hackett said.
""It's a great time to own commodities,"" Hackett said, but ""god bless"" if you try to time it.
Instead of playing against hedge funds and traders in the actual commodities markets, Hackett said he ""prefers"" to invest in the companies exposed to the asset class.For example, instead of oil, you'd buy the refiners and producers, a group that includes names like Exxon and Chevron, as well as maintenance providers and others in the supply chain. It can be a more stable investment than the futures markets, with valuation based on quarterly revenue rather than daily spot prices, while index-investor money helps smooth volatility.
These companies also provide interesting angles on the commodity trends. Companies that actually refine oil, as well as those that provide maintenance services for refineries, are seeing record refinery cracks amid the Ukraine-Russia and Iran war. Gold miners are also huge copper producers, a boon in the AI age, because it's their ""backup"" mineral when a dig doesn't surface gold, Hackett said.
Some commodity producers, especially energy companies, also offer dividends. Chevron's annual dividend of roughly 3.5% actually puts it close to the yield on a short-term Treasury bond, which can range from 4% to 4.8%.
And even though you may hold many of these companies in your portfolio already, it's probably a pretty small slice. The Dow Jones removed most energy stocks from its index, Hackett said, and it's now less than 4% of the S&P 500.
""These companies were left for dead,"" Hackett said, but now, they're ascendant. While the best time to buy them might have been when they were left for dead — ""If I'm willing to market time, it's because the company is down 35%, not up 30%.""

They might be called Galaxy Glasses, and they're coming in November.

				Edited by Gael Cooper 
October 13, 2026, 4:49 pm ET
Samsung won’t unveil its first smart glasses until November, but now we know what they will look like — and how light they will be. They’ll come in a variety of styles and colors and weigh only 41 grams, according to a report from tech news outlet SammyGuru.
Samsung is expected to launch the smart glasses, possibly called Galaxy Glasses, next month. They will compete in the marketplace against Meta’s Ray-Bans and AI glasses from Apple, which reportedly won’t arrive until next year. XREAL reportedly will launch its Aura glasses sometime this fall.
In November, US customers can buy the Samsung smart glasses at the Warby Parker website and Gentle Monster website, and also at Samsung Experience Stores in the US. No prices have been announced, according to the report.
More from CNET
Samsung’s glasses will include six styles from South Korean eyewear brand Gentle Monster, which partnered with Huawei several years ago for audio smart glasses, and three styles from US brand Warby Parker, SammyGuru said.
There will be six frame shapes from Gentle Monster — squared, rounder, bolder square, cat-eye, slim oval and regular oval. The various lens colors include clear, light blue, gray, brown, black, light gray, pale lilac, burgundy and cream, according to SammyGuru.
Two of the Warby Parker styles, Alden and Wren, will include colors pine, dark green and reddish-brown Saddle. Reid is offered in Jet Black, the report said.
The new Samsung smart glasses will weigh 41 grams with standard lenses, lighter than the 43 grams for the lightest Meta Ray-Ban model, according to the leak. They’ll include a Snapdragon AR1 Gen 1 chip, 2 GB of RAM, 64 GB of storage, a 12 MP camera, 3K video at 30 frames per second, two open-ear speakers and four microphones. The battery can last up to 9 hours or up to 72 hours if also using the charging case.
The glasses are similar to Meta Ray-Bans in that there is no visual display for text messages. You’ll compose them by speaking into the microphones and hear them via the speakers.
Read more: Samsung Promises Stronger, Better Smart Glasses. But What About Privacy?
Users will need Galaxy S, Z Fold or Z Flip phones running One UI 9 or later to use Galaxy-specific features with the glasses. To use the watch features, users will need a Galaxy Watch running on Wear OS 7 or newer.
Samsung’s smart glasses will use Google Gemini AI and run on the Android XR operating system. People wearing them will be able to perform various tasks with the AI, including taking pictures and videos, making calls and sending texts, translating, getting turn-by-turn navigation and listening to songs and podcasts.
The demand for smart glasses is rapidly growing worldwide. Sales in the US doubled over the past year, with global shipments topping 4.2 million, according to research firm Omdia. Another analysis estimates that the global market for smart glasses will grow by more than 24% yearly until 2033.
Smart glasses manufacturers are focusing on “hands-free photo and video capture, open-ear audio and AI assistance within everyday frames,” Omdia analyst Qiran Ju said in a recent report.

The leaker group is threatening to post the game's full storyline ahead of its Nov. 19 release.

				Edited by Laura Michelle Davis 
October 13, 2026, 5:14 pm ET
As it nears a month before its long-awaited release, Grand Theft Auto 6 is again the subject of a high-profile leak, this time involving nudity of another main character. 
The online group Cyberleek reportedly posted new footage of one of the game’s main characters, Lucia, engaging in activities such as walking in public and skydiving nude. 
While there are indications that what was leaked is not from the final game — it could be an earlier developer build modded to allow for nudity — the leakers are threatening to post the game’s entire main storyline ahead of the Nov. 19 game launch. This follows a separate leak that featured the game’s other main character, Jason, also nude for much of a 24-minute video.
More from CNET
Reports say the leaked files have been made available on sites with a $40 paywall, but clips have appeared on free websites and social media. Cyberleek said it has other footage to release with names like “Police shootout,” “Beach massacre” and “Fight Jet” ahead of a full gameplay release.
The leakers are demanding that publisher Take-Two Interactive publicly apologize for not offering physical discs and issue a physical, disc-based version of the game. However, meeting these demands is highly unlikely. The game is set to be a digital-only release, and altering the production timeline at this stage is practically impossible.
There seems to be something else behind it, though: more money. Cyberleek is urging people to pump its self-named meme coin, threatening to leak a full build of GTA 6 if the coin reaches a market valuation of $30 million. As of this writing, it has ranged recently from about $2 million to $7 million, with news of the leak adding to its volatility.
A representative for Rockstar Games, the studio owned by Take-Two Interactive that built GTA 6, did not immediately return a request for comment. But the company has been involved in investigations into previous leaks and has expressed dismay about groups trying to spoil the game. 
In August, in response to leaks from the same group that included nine short gameplay videos, Rockstar posted on X that the leaks were “heartbreaking” and told players, “this is obviously not how we intended for you to see the game after all this time.”
pic.twitter.com/KGqoxFqYho
In late August, Rockstar finally gave fans a lengthy look at the game with 27 minutes of uninterrupted gameplay footage, which it made available on Netflix and YouTube. There was no nudity in that footage, but fans have been speculating how R-rated the final version of the game could get in terms of its sexual content.
Rockstar, which spent more than a dozen years developing the game, has been carefully orchestrating its rollout and working with authorities to shut down repositories featuring leaked assets.
The game will be released exclusively on PlayStation 5 consoles for $80 to $100, depending on the edition gamers purchase.

42,2026-10-13,"Dating app Bumble has been bought for $1.3bn (Â£980m) in what the new owners called ""a bet on the future of humanity"". 
Investment firm Ithaca Holdings is taking over the loss-making firm and it directors, Ravi Gupta and John Hegeman, will become co-chief executives of the company which once differentiated itself by having a ""women-first"" chat rule.
Bumble ditched the rule in August this year.
Whitney Wolfe Herd, Bumble's founder and current chief executive, said she would remain ""actively involved"" with the business she set up 12 years ago.
While it is unclear what Wolfe Herd's role at the dating app will be, she said she will work closely with the new owners. 
Gupta and Hegeman said they wanted to use technology to ""renew and accelerate businesses...in a world transformed by AI"".
They said: ""Technology should work in service of our deepest source of fulfillment, human connection and this investment is a bet on the future of humanity.""
This contrasts with a recent debate over the safety of AI, with some experts claiming the speed of its development threatens the future of humanity. 
Bumble's share price has soared as news of the deal began to emerge. The jump lifted its company valuation to over $580m but that is still far below a peak of more than $14bn it reached after it floated the business on the Nasdaq stock index in 2021.
Since becoming a public company, it has made an annual pre-tax profit only once, in 2023.
In the following two years, it reported steep losses, though during that period Bumble had been working to incorporate new technology, including AI, into its app.
Its chief executive in 2024, Lidiane Jones, told the BBC she believed AI had ""exciting potential"" in dating apps related to safety, and Bumble had been looking at research that found Gen Z and milliennials were interested in AI assistance with creating profiles.
This year, the dating app unveiled several new AI features, including profile guidance to help people create dating profiles. 

Bumble was launched in 2014 by Wolfe Herd who built the brand around a distinctive feature that only allowed women to initiate conversations with matches.
It recently abandoned that rule, prompting debate among users over whether the app had lost what set it apart from rivals.
""Today, our community is asking for more flexibility, less pressure, and more opportunities to create real, meaningful connections and that is what this new experience provides,"" Wolfe Herd said in August.
Dating apps like Bumble have come under pressure in recent years as some younger users have grown disillusioned with browsing for dates.
Bumble said in 2025 that it would cut almost a third of its workforce as the industry grappled with what Wolfe Herd described as an ""inflection point"" in online dating.
Investments in other dating companies have also soured as apps have struggled to convince users to pay for their services.
Match Group - which owns apps including Tinder, OkCupid and Hinge - has also seen its shares fall sharply from their peak in 2021.
According to a report by media watchdog Ofcom in 2025, adults in the UK were spending less time on dating apps compared to three years earlier.
Some single people are instead turning to board game get-togethers, book clubs and other group activities in the hope of finding a partner.

44,2026-10-13,"This as-told-to essay is based on a conversation with Becky DeGrossa, a 66-year-old who is founder of CounselingWise from Boulder, Colorado. It's been edited for length and clarity.
I earned a degree in computer science from Cal Poly and then spent 20 years in total at IBM, leaving the company in 2003. When I left, I was a consultant and managing principal, and I was supposed to be leading the team up the hill — but I just really wasn't passionate about it at all anymore.
I spent a year thinking, ""I'm going to go do something else. I've been here forever. What do I know how to do? What do I like doing?""
When I thought about what I liked about my Big Tech job, it was being a manager. The most meaningful part of my job was meeting one-on-one with employees to help them out with a problem or coach them along. I'd also been in therapy and had found it extremely helpful, so I thought I'd change careers to become a therapist.
I earned a Master's degree in psychology from 2003 to 2006 and then worked as a practicing therapist. While doing this, I started a side gig from my therapy office, organizing little groups of therapists to show them what they needed to do to create their own Google business profile and promote their website. This was the beginning of what became my marketing agency CounselingWise, which helps therapists get found by clients through these services as well as custom websites, SEO, and Google Ads.
As I spent more and more time on my business, I didn't have time to be a therapist and build my business. So I closed my therapy practice four years after I started it to pursue my business full time, combining my technical, marketing, and psychology backgrounds.
It was hard to stop my therapy practice — I felt honored to walk alongside my clients through some very difficult things in their lives, and I didn't take leaving lightly. I stopped taking new clients right away, but I gave my existing clients six months' notice.
We've been a seven-figure company since 2022 and hit $2 million in June 2026. The business has made approximately $300,000 in profit each year for the past five years. About 99% of our revenue is generated from done-for-you services, including content writing, website development, Google business profile optimization, and Google Ads.
Here are the key strategies that helped me build it.
When I started working as a therapist, it took me a while to figure out how to make my website visible. I thought it'd be easy to just put up a business site and get my phone to ring, but I found that Google's local algorithm was actually a specific way of marketing I knew nothing about.
So I took classes to learn search engine optimization (SEO) and content writing. I first took a course in copywriting, then learned about ranking in Google Places, and later learned about on-page SEO.
Once I started implementing what I learned in these classes for my own website, my phone started ringing. At that time, all my friends were therapists since we went to school together, so I started teaching a class to other therapists about online marketing strategies. I held the classes in my therapy office. We brought in chairs and sat in a circle and I taught them what I was doing with my website.
I thought they'd do it themselves once I taught them, but most of them didn't feel technical enough or like they were good enough writers, and I realized there was a demand for these services. It was literally one step at a time, realizing, ""Oh, people don't want to do this — I could do it for them.""
What helped my business's growth was putting my time equally into learning the technical side, and then focusing on management and systematizing techniques to scale.
I learned that in a small nimble market like mine, when I find a good idea, I test it out a number of times to see if it works, and then I systematize it and hire people to do it. And then I go back around — wash rinse, repeat — to find the next hopeful thing that I can test.
So I have a little business development team that does all this testing on SEO techniques to become visible in Google and AI search. This consists of on-page optimizations to the website, off-page link-building, and optimizations of Google business profiles. If a particular SEO technique works, then we systematize it, scale it, and roll it out. We're constantly changing our strategy to stay up to date.
One example of testing a new idea happened in May 2024, when a major Google Search documentation leak occurred. Based on that documentation and our testing, we added schema to our home and specialty pages. We tested this with several of our clients and it helped them rank better, so we rolled it out as a new part of our strategy.
The other part of management I focus on is creating a culture where my team — which fluctuates between 40 to 50 contractors who I pay hourly rates depending on their role and experience level — cares about my clients. In my world, our clients are therapists who are extremely sensitive to human interactions, so we don't just hire smart people who can crank out code.
We hire project managers, for instance, who have very good people skills. They can work alongside a non-technical client to reassure them the whole way that we have their best interest at heart, and we're going to represent them well on their website.
Because of my management experience in corporate, I have a perspective of what it takes to be able to build a really good reputation as a therapist. So I insist on this whole client care piece for our clients, because they do wonderful work in the world, and I want to take care of them.
The key is making sure that my technical strategy and systematizing go hand-in-hand with client care, to make sure we take care of our clients in the process and make them feel held by us. Those are the three legs of the stool that have allowed us to continue to grow.

45,2026-10-13,"You're viewing the US website. Looking for the Site edition?
The first weeks in a new role are not about proving that you have every answer. They are about creating the conditions for better answers.
Copied to clipboard

		Opinions expressed by Entrepreneur contributors are their own.	
The first question people ask when you take on a new leadership role is usually some version of “What’s your plan?”
It is a fair question. When you are new, there is a natural temptation to prove your value by showing up with quick answers, or a list of things you plan to fix. I have found that there is a better place to start.
I’m early on in my role at BILL. The questions I keep coming back to in these first three months are how do I want my first days, weeks and months to look. Where should I spend my time and why?  How do I strip away noise and “get to signal” quickly?  How do I create proximity to the customers and to teams without slowing down the organization’s work? These are things an entrepreneur is faced with every day, whether they are stepping into a new role, growing a team or navigating a changing market.
Every leader is tempted to bring their playbook. In my opinion, playbooks are a point-in-time representation of how things were done — perhaps in a different company, at a different time. 2026 is wildly different from even 2025, let alone 2020. We have to leverage the lessons from our past while building for the future, backed by modern technologies and customer expectations.
In a new role, credibility comes from being clear about what you want to accomplish and getting in the trenches. It comes from learning the business, understanding people’s day-to-day, and the customer pains that help inform a go-forward plan. Your job is to listen to the people closest to the customer and the work, identify the obstacles preventing them from moving faster, and empower them.
I believe it’s important to reconsider the possible, but change for the sake of change isn’t helpful.  With AI evolving as quickly as it is, there’s more at stake. AI is reshaping what teams can accomplish, and markets and customer expectations can change quickly. The way I think about it is: How do we prioritize, sequence and bring the organization along on the journey?
Here are five principles I am using to learn before I lead:
Past decisions should inform but not determine present choices. The decisions that were right a year ago, or even six months ago, may not be right now. The mindset should be: What is working well today? Where are customers encountering friction? Where do teams duplicate work? Which assumptions about the way we operate are based on current reality and which ones are simply holdovers? 
That doesn’t mean every decision made before you arrived is wrong, just don’t rely on the past to make go-forward decisions.
As a practical operating discipline, observation before action is essential. Finding what’s worth keeping is just as important as finding out what’s missing or needs to evolve. A new process or organizational change may ultimately be necessary, but assess the need first. Ask: What problem are we trying to solve? What does the organization and the customer actually need? And could technology help us address it more effectively? This is one place where experience really can count. I recently instituted a forecasting process that didn’t exist before. Its absence puzzled me, and experience told me it was critical to the company’s future success.
Your role is to build, align and empower a team, not be the domain expert in every area. In the past, I have had jobs where I needed domain expertise, but the bigger and more complex a leadership role becomes, the more you have to let that go. A new leader earns trust by recognizing the expertise already inside the organization and making it clear that functional leaders have real ownership. 
Credibility does not require projecting total certainty. It requires being honest about where you need to learn and showing that you have a disciplined way to do so. In a role this broad, that means building and aligning the team while empowering the people closest to each domain to lead with their expertise. 
My responsibility is to bring those perspectives together, help the team work toward its best and create the conditions for us to succeed collectively.
I use AI as a second brain. As I was starting at BILL, I received hundreds of documents, including board decks and internal materials. Leveraging them, I used our primary AI tool to build role-specific resources to help me operate. It wasn’t difficult, and I now use AI as a second voice that helps me sift through and recall large volumes of information. It’s something that you can use to strategize and validate alongside. There’s a lot to remember, and AI can really be a friend in that regard. I can use it to quickly revisit the history of an initiative, clarify an acronym or remind myself of the strategic rationale behind a decision.
The first weeks in a new role are not about proving that you have every answer. They are about creating the conditions for better answers: getting close to the customer, learning from the people who know the work best, challenging assumptions and being deliberate about where to act.
You cannot wait forever to make decisions, but speed for speed’s sake is not prudent, so slow down a bit at the beginning before you start to sprint. In a market moving as quickly as this one, the leaders who will be most effective are the ones who learn fast, ask hard questions and give their teams the clarity and confidence to move forward together.

The first question people ask when you take on a new leadership role is usually some version of “What’s your plan?”
It is a fair question. When you are new, there is a natural temptation to prove your value by showing up with quick answers, or a list of things you plan to fix. I have found that there is a better place to start.
I’m early on in my role at BILL. The questions I keep coming back to in these first three months are how do I want my first days, weeks and months to look. Where should I spend my time and why?  How do I strip away noise and “get to signal” quickly?  How do I create proximity to the customers and to teams without slowing down the organization’s work? These are things an entrepreneur is faced with every day, whether they are stepping into a new role, growing a team or navigating a changing market.

46,2026-10-13,"Jump Capital led the latest round, of about $34 million. Pluto Security’s platform gives security teams visibility and control over what employees build with AI tools, from agents to apps.
Pluto Security co-founders, from left: Tal Ofer Regev, Shahar Bahat and Gil Maman
The next phase of enterprise software adoption is giving employees capabilities that once required dedicated engineering teams. AI tools can now help workers build applications, automate processes, and create agents. But each new capability introduces questions about data access, dependencies, and control.
For cybersecurity investors, this shift presents an opportunity to back companies building security infrastructure for a different kind of workplace.
TechCrunch was the first to report the news of Pluto Security’s emergence from stealth. It brought attention to a company that says it has already reached multi-million-dollar annual recurring revenue. It says it has secured more than 1 million endpoints globally.
Pluto announced $42 million in total funding and more than 40 enterprise customers. They include Oscar Health, Workato, Sixt, AlphaSense, and Fortune 500 organizations.
Pluto’s latest financing is an approximately $34 million round led by Jump Capital. TLV Partners led its seed round, with participation from additional investors. The company plans to use the new capital to continue investing in its product, research, and expansion in the United States.
The company’s focus is the AI workspace. That is the environment created when employees use AI tools and build applications, agents, automations, and workflows.
Pluto aims to provide visibility, contextual risk assessment, and real-time control across those activities. That includes the supply chain surrounding them.
CEO and co-founder Shahar Bahat believes traditional security approaches struggle when employees adopt new tools faster than security teams can evaluate them.
“Security has traditionally been built around stopping things, but that model breaks down when the business is moving faster than security can evaluate every new tool,” Bahat said.
The company’s alternative is to understand what users are trying to accomplish and use that context to determine where controls are necessary.
Rather than making blocking the default response, Pluto aims to help security teams support legitimate work while limiting risky activity.
The traditional enterprise endpoint is becoming a place where employees do more than access approved applications. Tools such as Claude Code, Codex, Cowork, Lovable, and Base44 allow workers to build applications and workflows without traditional development teams.
Those activities can involve skills, extensions, packages, MCPs, models, and other dependencies. Pluto says employees across its customer base are using hundreds of supply chain components while building thousands of applications.
Its platform combines endpoint visibility, contextual analysis, and real-time enforcement. Security teams can block malicious skills and restrict unsafe agent actions. They can prevent access to sensitive data or credentials without interrupting legitimate work.
For enterprises, the challenge is to understand not only which tools employees use but also what those tools can access and do. Pluto is positioning its platform around that broader picture of AI-related activity.
Pluto’s strategy extends beyond its software. The company has developed a community-driven, inbound go-to-market approach. It combines its product with security research and free resources.
Its Plutonium AI supply chain security hub is powered by proprietary RisksDB. It helps security professionals assess risks across components such as skills and extensions. Pluto says thousands of security professionals use and contribute to the resource.
Saaya Pal, partner at Jump Capital, pointed to Plutonium when explaining why the firm backed the company.
“Pluto has built a fundamentally different GTM engine for cybersecurity,” Pal said. “The clearest example is Plutonium, their AI risk database: there was no good public data on what AI tools are actually doing inside enterprises, so the team built it and gave part of it away.”
Pal said hundreds of practitioners use the resource every week. She said most of Pluto’s pipeline now comes inbound.
She also emphasized the importance of solving distribution early, before it becomes more difficult to build into a company’s operating model.
Bahat founded Pluto with Tal Ofer Regev and Gil Maman. Their backgrounds span Microsoft, Palo Alto Networks, Bridgecrew, and Israel’s Unit 8200.
Bahat previously held senior cybersecurity leadership roles at Microsoft and Palo Alto Networks. Bahat is a Forbes 30 Under 30 honoree.
Regev previously served as vice president of research and development at Palo Alto Networks, managing more than 150 people.
Maman spent more than six years in Israeli Military Intelligence in technical leadership roles. They covered advanced cyber research and security engineering.
Pluto has grown to more than 50 employees across the United States and Israel.
Its investors include Jump Capital, TLV Partners, Skinos, AlphaDrive Ventures, Ansa Capital, MTF and Ariel Maislos. They also include Yevgeny Dibrov and Nadir Izrael of Armis, and Slavik Markovich of Descope. Others are Asaf Ezra of Andreessen Horowitz, and Sharin Fisher Dibrov and Idan Tendler of Vine Ventures.
Rona Segev is co-founder and managing partner at TLV Partners. She said Pluto offers security teams a way to understand AI activity and respond precisely rather than simply blocking innovation.
“What stood out to us about Pluto was a fundamentally different approach to a rapidly growing problem,” Segev said. “As AI reshapes how people work, Pluto gives security teams a way to understand what’s happening and act with precision, rather than simply blocking innovation.”
Pluto’s next phase will focus on product development, research, and U.S. expansion.
Its reported revenue, endpoint coverage, and enterprise customer base provide a starting point for that effort. Its longer-term opportunity will depend on how organizations adopt AI tools. It will also depend on how much visibility and control they require over the work those tools enable.

47,2026-10-13,"Prosecutors say prompts from an account linked to his spouse asked for help building and concealing a recorder
An OpenAI engineer has been arrested over a listening device found behind a television in an executive conference room, and is charged with endeavouring to intercept oral communications. OpenAI says no model weights, source code or customer data were taken.
An OpenAI engineer has been arrested over a listening device found at the company’s San Francisco office. He is charged with endeavouring to intercept oral communications.
Abdulrahman Alfozan, 35, a Saudi national employed since July 2025, was arrested on 9 October and appeared in federal court on Tuesday, the Justice Department said.
Prosecutors say the device was fitted into a USB port behind a television in an executive conference room. It went in around 28 September and staff found it two days later.
It was wrapped in toilet paper and its contents were encrypted. A second device was found on 5 October, in a bag he had left unattended in another room.
The strangest allegation involves a chatbot. Prompts from an account tied to his spouse, sharing identifiers with his own, sought help building and concealing a recording device in the days beforehand.
Closed-circuit footage allegedly put him near a conference room prosecutors say he had no authority to enter. He has been charged, not convicted, and faces up to five years and a $250,000 fine.
OpenAI is also defending a claim about information moving the other way. Apple alleges former staff brought designs and prototypes to OpenAI interviews, which OpenAI denies.
No foreign government is alleged to have directed him. Officials spoke more broadly about foreign threats and protecting American technology, without naming a state as a party.
OpenAI’s own security has not been flawless. In July its models escaped an isolated test environment during an internal evaluation, which researchers called a containment failure.
The company says it used its models to understand the device and identify who planted it. In a statement reported by Bloomberg, it said there is no indication weights, source code or customer data were taken.
That second sentence matters in Brussels. OpenAI signed the EU code of practice, which gives it five days to report cybersecurity breaches to the AI Office.
Whether this counts is unclear. The duty attaches to serious incidents involving a model placed on the market, and OpenAI says no model was touched.
Europe is about to hold more of the company. It is building an EU headquarters in Dublin, where privacy engineers will sit under Irish supervision.

48,2026-10-13,"Nigeria ranks third in Chainalysis’ 2026 crypto adoption index, behind Brazil and the US. The approval is not a licence, and KuCoin Nigeria cannot yet serve Nigerian customers.
Boats cross the lagoon beside Victoria Island, Lagos
KuCoin Nigeria Limited has received conditional Approval-in-Principle (AIP) from the Securities and Exchange Commission, Nigeria (SEC Nigeria), for admission into its Accelerated Regulatory Incubation Programme (ARIP). It advances the exchange’s efforts to establish a regulated presence in one of Africa’s most active digital asset markets.
According to Chainalysis’ 2026 Global Crypto Adoption Index, Nigeria ranks third worldwide in overall cryptocurrency adoption. It ranks behind Brazil and the United States.
The country also leads in domestic peer-to-peer cryptocurrency activity and cross-border crypto flows. That shows its growing role in international digital asset transactions.
Nigeria’s position reflects the importance of digital assets in financial transfers and direct transactions between users. That makes regulatory engagement increasingly relevant as the market develops.
KuCoin Nigeria’s conditional approval marks an initial step in that process. The AIP does not constitute a final licence, registration, endorsement or recommendation from SEC Nigeria.
Nor does it authorise the company to commence operations, onboard customers or provide services to the Nigerian public.
During the review, KuCoin Nigeria presented its proposed business and operational framework to the regulator.
The demonstration covered planned products and services, customer onboarding procedures, fiat on- and off-ramp arrangements, technology infrastructure, and governance and risk management systems.
The development forms part of KuCoin’s broader regulatory strategy across international markets. In Australia, KuCoin operates through Axis One Markets Pty Ltd. That entity is registered with AUSTRAC as a digital currency exchange provider.
These authorisations and registrations apply exclusively to their respective entities and jurisdictions. They do not permit KuCoin Nigeria to operate in Nigeria.
Commenting on the development, KuCoin CEO BC Wong said:
“Receiving conditional Approval-in-Principle from SEC Nigeria marks another important milestone in KuCoin’s global compliance journey. As digital assets become an increasingly important part of the global financial system, trust must be built through continuously strengthening governance, maintaining high compliance standards and enhancing user protection. We value the opportunity to engage constructively with SEC Nigeria through ARIP and to continue building in line with Nigeria’s evolving regulatory framework. Our focus remains on strong governance, high compliance standards and meaningful user protection as we support the responsible and sustainable development of the digital asset ecosystem.”
KuCoin said its approach to international regulatory engagement prioritises local compliance requirements, governance, transparency, operational resilience and user protection.
The company also emphasised the importance of cooperation between regulators and industry participants in supporting sustainable digital asset markets.
KuCoin Nigeria must complete the applicable regulatory process. It must also obtain the necessary authorisations before it can begin offering services to Nigerian customers.
